4.8. Smart Contract

A contract, as we all know, is a legal bond between two parties. A smart contract refers to a piece of code that is stored on the Blockchain network and executed automatically when certain conditions are met. Colloquially, they are self-executing scripts encoding set protocols in Blockchain that digitally verify and implement the contract made between participants and ultimately help in transferring digital assets [10]. Smart contracts were first introduced by Ethereum and are written using the Solidity programming language. There are many tools available for authoring Smart Contracts, but the web-based tool Remix is the handiest among all.

Let’s quickly perform a fun activity by creating and deploying a smart contract on an Ethereum test network. For this, we will first download the browser extension of MetaMask. MetaMask is a browser plugin that serves as an Ethereum Browser as well as a wallet. Once downloaded, we need to create a wallet. We can use any one of the test Networks available to get some Dummy Ethers in our wallet because the deployment of a smart contract cannot be done for free. (Ether is the cryptocurrency of Ethereum). For this, we need to select one of the test networks available as shown in Fig. (4) (I have used the Goerli Test Network because it is comparatively easier to get test Ethers on this network, but you can choose any) and open the Testnet Faucet browser of that network. By providing our account address, we can request them to give us test Ethers.

Having done this, we will open the remix.ethereum.org webpage and create our contract using the solidity language. Let us build a smart contract for the electricity bill. For this, we will create a new file with the ‘.sol’ extension and follow the steps shown in Figs. (5 and 6).

5. FEATURES

5.1. Distributed

It is a distributed technology that allows data/transactions to be synchronized and shared among all entities in the chain instead of being copied or transferred. What needs to be understood here is that due to the absence of a single data storehouse and transactions being distributed among all the entities, the records/ money can never be hacked or stolen. This also prevents load on a single server by distributing the computational power among the many computers on the network. Advantages:

  • Better and more efficient outcome due to distributed computational power
  • Ownership to all members is assured because they actively participate in all the transactions.
  • Since the replica of each transaction gets stored on all the nodes, tampering with the data is impossible because any fraudulent activity can easily be detected.
Fig. (6))
Transaction details in the Etherscan Network.

5.2. Decentralized

It means that there is no need for a governing authority or third party to manage the data and transactions. This implies that it is an open ledger technology and ensures full integrity among all the members who are a part of the blockchain because every entity in the chain has the same copy of the ledger. By this feature, the users remain super active, alert, and always updated regarding all the transactions that take place [11]. To list a few advantages of these features:

  • No need for third parties to drastically increases the speed of the transaction process
  • Data will never be lost because each user has a copy of it
  • Lower transaction cost
  • No possibility of scams

Note: The two terms mentioned above might seem similar in context but in reality, they are very different. Decentralization relates to the level of control and the authority they share in decision-making whereas distributed network refers to the difference in location of the users. In a decentralized network, the decision is made based on the voting done by all the nodes. As stated earlier, if at least 51% of the participants vote in favor of something, that action is performed, or else it is rejected. But in a distributed system, the processing is shared among all the participants, but it might allow the decision to be made exclusively by a central authority. Blockchain incorporates both these features.

5.3. Immutable

Data once added to the Blocks can never be changed because it gets permanently stored on the Blockchain network forever. This means that data that was first added to the Bitcoin network when it was created is still safe and secure and can easily be accessed. What makes it different from Databases regarding this feature is that Databases allow us to Update and Delete data stored on their network, in addition to the creation and addition of data. However, this is not the case in Blockchain. While it allows us to Create and Add data, Updating and Deletion is not possible. It adds to its security because transaction records since Day 1 are stored just as they are and any attempt to tamper with the data goes in vain. Both the sender and receiver can rest assured that their data will never be meddled with.

5.4. Transparent

The addition of a single block or transaction between any two persons is reflected on all the blocks. Thus, nothing is hidden from anybody and changes are viewable publicly. It is one advantageous feature. Let’s understand how. Imagine that a fraudulent participant has gained illegal access to the network and is pretending to be some other person. He can just not make any transaction because firstly, it needs to be validated by all, and secondly and most importantly, the private key is a unique key which is almost impossible to guess correctly, and in case a person tries a few keys, all these attempts are reflected on all the blocks and he can easily be traced.

5.5. Peer to Peer Network

Blockchain allows transactions to occur securely between two parties excluding the need of any third party. Each transaction is signed digitally by using some sort of Cryptographic Algorithm. This lets only the true receiver decipher the code with the knowledge of the sender’s public key and his private key. Hashing algorithms used for signature generation are of immense importance because they act like a trapdoor and make them practically impossible for a cybercriminal to crack it.

6. TYPES AND PLATFORMS

This technology can be divided into 3 types [12]:

6.1. Public Blockchain

As the name suggests, this type is open to use by the general public and is not owned by any single authority. Anyone can participate in the consensus method of decision-making. All participants are given equal authority to decide the fate of the ledger by following the 51% rule, according to which if 51% of the participants vote in favor of a decision, for example, creation of a block, it is created, otherwise, the proposal is discarded. Each user actively participates in the transaction verification process. The users may or might not be rewarded for their involvement. To understand this clearly, imagine that you are a part of a public Blockchain network. A request for someone’s new transaction pops up on your screen. To verify it, you are supposed to solve some mathematical problems and if you are the first one to give the correct answer, you are rewarded some money in terms of online Blockchain-based currency. The answers given by the other participants are equally important for decision-making, but they are not always rewarded. This type of Blockchain can be used to conduct votes during elections or in maintaining cryptocurrencies.

6.2. Private Blockchain

Private Blockchain, just like its name, are private. The accessibility and transactions are restricted to the members of the chain. Members may include organizations or some individuals who share personal data among themselves. The authority on data management is strictly monitored and controlled. This type of Blockchain might be used for some private bank, or a particular company to maintain their business records or a university to maintain the records of its students, staff, official documents, etc. It cannot be accessed by the general public. Platforms include R3 Corda, Ripple

6.3. Consortium Blockchain

It is also known as a semi-private or hybrid blockchain and incorporates both public and private blockchain networks. It can be perceived as a semi-decentralized network. It can be hosted on a private network where a few nodes can form a public blockchain just to incorporate its best features of stability, security, and verification. This Blockchain might be used for storing data of a mobile company for example, for which read-only permission is granted to the public but read and write access is provided to a few selected employees. Some notable platforms are Hyperledger, MultiChain, etc.

Following Table 3 briefly compares the three types of Blockchain:

Table 3 Comparison of the three types of blockchain.
Property Public Blockchain Private Blockchain Consortium Blockchain
Access Open Limited to members Partly open
Transparency Completely transparent Transparent only to users Depends upon set rules
Decentralization Yes No Partly
Reward Participants rewarded for growing the chain No incentives known Might or might not reward participants (depends on set rules)
Speed Comparatively slow Faster Somewhere in between
Efficiency Low High High
Consensus Determination All miners Selected nodes One authority
Use case Almost every industry like cryptocurrency, supply-chain management, stock market, etc. For organizations who need to keep their data private or carry out personal tasks For organizations who want to make the best use of public blockchain features but want to keep their information completely private.
Platform Bitcoin, Ethereum Corda, Ripple Hyperledger

7. HOW DOES IT WORK?

Having studied the basic elements of a Blockchain, we now know that all records in the ledger are stored in blocks that are secured by digital signatures made using cryptographic functions. Blocks are intangible and immutable. New blocks are created only when the old one runs out of memory. In a public Blockchain, when a request for new data to be added to the blockchain is received, the request is reflected on all the nodes in the network. If the transaction is approved following the consensus rules, it is validated by adding a timestamp and added to the blockchain network by linking it to the previous block. This decision as to which nodes will append the block is very critical because cybercriminals might be ready and lucky enough to cause the 51% attack. For this reason, the mechanism of the consensus algorithm being used also becomes extremely important. A transaction is made secure following the cryptographic algorithms. The main steps of how a Blockchain transaction is approved/ carried out are listed below:

  • Step 1: Transaction request is reflected on all nodes in the network.
  • Step 2: Consensus mechanisms are followed for its approval/ rejection.
  • Step 3: Transaction is approved and validated.
  • Step 4: Block gets added to the chain and the transaction is carried out.

8. APPLICATIONS, ADVANTAGES & CHALLENGES

8.1. APPLICATIONS

With its features and ability to carry out transactions within a short period, Blockchain is one such technology that can blend with almost all the technologies out there [13]. It is slowly impacting many different sectors, thereby making their task more efficient and secure. A few applications are listed below:

  1. Blockchain-based Cryptocurrency- The most well-known example being Bitcoin, other cryptocurrencies like Litecoin and Ethers are also being developed. This can be attributed to the distributed, decentralization, immutability, and transparency features of this technology. DCEP by the Central Bank of China was launched on 29th August 2020 to develop an electronic cash infrastructure by providing anonymous digital wallets with no bank account [14].
  2. Secure sharing of Medical data- Healthcare is one sector where reliable activity tracking is needed and records need to be maintained perfectly. Introducing Blockchain technology in this field will make the best use of its potential and drastically improve healthcare facilities [15-18].
  3. In Supply Chain Management- Here Blockchain is needed to provide secure and complete information and to maintain proper integrity between the members, who need to be updated regularly about the products being developed [19].
  4. In Business Applications- It helps save time and money and ensures proper management and safe monitoring of each step of progress [20].
  5. In Elections- In democratic countries where elections take place to elect their leaders, Blockchain can be used to carry out fair elections where all records are maintained easily and the personal identity of the voters is not revealed [21].
  6. Government-based application for storing personal documents- Governments can use Blockchain platforms to store and exchange the personal documents of the people. Safe storage of these documents, especially in places that are prone to annual calamities like floods, is immensely helpful because the document is stored safely forever and can never be tampered with.

8.2. ADVANTAGES

The features of Blockchain technology already discussed in section 5 of this chapter can also be taken as its advantages. A few advantages have already been mentioned there. However, if you sit and think about it, you will realize that there are many more advantages to this technology that is reshaping the financial world. A few other advantages are mentioned below:

  1. The biggest advantage is that it addresses the privacy and security concerns of the users by using cryptographic hash functions.
  2. Authentic transactions: Data once entered cannot be deleted or tampered with and also every block contains a permanent time-stamp of the transactions. This makes the whole process as authentic as any other and adds to the security and privacy features.
  3. Faster speed and Increased efficiency: Because the entire system is decentralized and distributed and not dependent on some central authority for transactions or data storage, the speed of the business being carried out drastically increases and the efficiency improves. This also means that time taken for one transaction reduces which is a major advantage because of time constraints in recent times.
  4. Easy to use: We got a brief exposure to the deployment of smart contracts in the network and saw how easy it is to work on this platform. We can safely carry out our transactions from anywhere and anytime, unlike financial organizations that have time restrictions.
  5. Improved records and Better Traceability: As each block contains metadata including the Block version, timestamp, Merkle Tree Root Hash, Hash of parent block, bits, and Nonce (Number used only once), it becomes very easy and hassle-free to trace them even if they were generated 10 years ago. The fact that Blocks created since day 1 include a timestamp and are all intact and can never be deleted or meddled with indicates that the records are all maintained in chronological order. This advantage can, among others, best be put to use in the healthcare sector where a proper record of a patient’s medical history needs to be stored.
  6. Since transactions are added to a block only after the process of maximum trust verification by using consensus protocols to validate the entry, it eliminates risks like double-entry which can cause double payments to occur.

8.3. CHALLENGES

There is still a lot of excitement in the tech world about Blockchain Technology, and the excitement is only increasing every day. This technology is no doubt the most influential and thrilling in the 21st century, and we have already gone through quite a few applications and advantages of this technology. However, this technology is still in its developmental phase because of the few challenges it brings along with it, some of which are mentioned below:

  1. Scalability- This is one of the biggest challenges, especially in public blockchains, which need to be resolved soon. Storage requirement has become an issue. Let us understand this with the help of the Bitcoin example. In Bitcoin, the size of one block is restricted to 1 MB and in general, a block is mined almost every 10 min. Also, a Bitcoin network has a limitation of 7 transactions/second, which is not capable of dealing with high-frequency trade. Also, larger blocks require more storage space and a slower rate of propagation [22]. This has the possibility of causing a shift to centralized networks that can maintain such a huge chain of transactions. This is causing a trade-off between security and block size, which is a major concern [23, 24].
  2. Limited Supply to increase demand- The total number of Bitcoins which will ever be available is 21 million. It has been predicted that the last of them will be mined in approximately 2140, and we have already mined 17 million bitcoins to date.
  3. Energy Consumption- This technology uses the Proof-of-Work mechanism for proper validation of the transactions and the ultimate addition to the network. This process uses a lot of computational power to reflect transactions on every node, solve complex mathematical problems required for the processing, and verification of transactions [25].
  4. Interoperability- Most Blockchain networks work in silos and are incapable of exchanging information with other Blockchain networks that are not a part of them. It can be speculated as one of the major reasons why most organizations are still not making a move towards this technology. Though attempts have been made towards resolving this issue, it has not helped much and a major portion of this problem still needs to be fixed.
  5. Lack of trained professionals is yet another challenge, but that is gradually being fixed with increasing awareness regarding this topic.

9. FUTURE SCOPE

Though the pioneer implementer of this technology was the financial sector, it is expected to be adopted gradually by many other sectors. Some challenges need to be resolved, and it is expected to pave the way for new opportunities for scientists and researchers who work in this field. It is anticipated that the integration of this technology with other technologies like the Internet of Things, Artificial Intelligence, and Machine Learning will bring about incredible changes across the globe. Also, there is no standardization in terms of the Blockchain mechanisms followed and there are hundreds of different Blockchain platforms out there. Therefore, if a company wants to incorporate this technology in their workspace, they first need to find out which platform best suits their requirement. They can be done by Blockchain Testing wherein different parameters with special emphasis on the function to be performed is tested to check if the platform works as claimed by the developers [26, 27].

CONCLUSION

Blockchain technology can be perceived as a distributed decentralized ledger technology that is transparent and immutable and stores records as transactions in time-stamped blocks. It allows the users to make and verify transactions fast without the need for any central authority. Though this technology is still in its infancy and its first applications, starting with the development of Bitcoin, and limited to the financial sector, it is slowly creeping into all other sectors and has the potential to transform the traditional industries with its features. When clubbed with other technologies like the Internet of Things, Artificial Intelligence, and Machine Learning, it can bring about spectacularly impressive changes that add to the modernization of human lives. Having developed a fundamental idea about what Blockchain is and also its applications, advantages, challenges, and future trends, we can conclude the chapter with the famous actor Fred Ehrsam’s remark, “While it may seem scarier in the earlier days, I think ultimately the blockchain creates a safer world.”

Industrial Revolution: Blockchain as a Wave for Industry 4.0 and IIoT



Sukriti Goyal1, Nikhil Sharma1, *, Ila Kaushik2, Bharat Bhushan3
1 HMR Institute of Technology & Management, Delhi, India
2 Krishna Institute of Engineering & Technology, Ghaziabad, U.P
3 School of Engineering and Technology, Sharda University, Greater Noida, India

Abstract

In ancient times, humans were much predisposed to utilizing their hand-created tools to finish any kind of work. From the last few years, businesses related to production have been expanding. People began to rely on tools, machines, and smart gadgets to process their work, as these kinds of tools help them to achieve their target in the given time. . Since the 18th century, the world has experienced a lot of revolutions in industry across the globe. This type of growth in technology has made a base for industrial revolutions. New technology was introduced called “blockchain.”Probably, blockchain technology is the most disruptive technology in the modern digital economy. The ability of blockchain technology has been greatly described in many research works, media, and majorly in the fields of finance and payment. One existing research is at the organizational stage, where it implements the architecture for internet safety as well as inconvertibility. “Industry 4.0” and “IIoT (Industrial Internet of Things)” are involved in its rising applications. Thus, in this paper, current applications of blockchain in IIoT as well as Industry 4.0 setups, existing open issues, modern application sectors, challenges related to IIoT, and their solutions are illustrated. The main focus of this paper is to empower and facilitate research in this field, which will help the developers in blockchain acquisition as well as investment in the “Industry 4.0” and “Industrial Internet of Things” space.

Keywords: Agriculture industry, Blockchain, Education industry, Food supply chain, Healthcare industry, Industrial revolution, Industry 4.0, IoT (Internet of Things), Privacy, Security, Scalability.

* Corresponding author Nikhil Sharma: HMR Institute of Technology & Management, Delhi, India; E-mail: nikhilsharma1694@gmail.com

1. INTRODUCTION

Undoubtedly, blockchain technology is an unpretentious invention. Blockchain structured the backbone of a modern kind of internet by permitting digital information to be disseminated but not replicated. Genuinely, invented for the digital currency, i.e., Bitcoin, the tech organization has now discovered other efficient usages of this technology [1]. Thus, in simple words, a blockchain is a time-stamped sequence of inconvertible collections of information that is handled by a network of computers and not owned by any individual entity [2]. Each of these digital blocks is defended and limited to each other by employing a public database (i.e., chain) [3]. In the network, by using cryptographic principles to keep data sharing safe, blockchain technology gives a decentralized database or digital ledger of transactions to everyone [4]. Basically, this network is a series of computer systems that must allow sharing before it can be authenticated and stored. There are many core elements of blockchain, but only the three most necessary elements are described. The first element is a block in which every chain includes several blocks, and each block has three fundamental components such as the data stored in the block is the first component [5]. A whole number of 32-bit is known as a nonce. In the second component, when a block is generated, it is randomly created which further then, produces a block header hash [6]. In third, a number of 256-bit called hash is wedded to the nonce. It must begin with a large number of 0s that is, it will be enormously small [7]. A nonce creates the cryptographic hash after the generation of the first block of a chain. The data in the block is considered as signed and forever linked to the nonce as well as hash until it is mined [8]. The second element is a miner. Through a procedure known as mining, miners produce fresh blocks on the chain. In a blockchain, every block has its own unrepeatable hash as well as a nonce, but also, it references the hash of the preceding block in the chain [9]. So, It is not simple to mine a block, specifically on huge chains. To solve the complicated math problem of finding a nonce that produces an admissible hash, special software is used by miners, because the nonce is only 32 bits and the hash is 256 bits. There are approximately 4 billion plausible combinations of nonce-hash that must be mined just before searching the correction [10]. When that occurs, miners have to search the “golden nonce” and their data is included in the database. Any kind of modification is accepted by all the nodes of the network and the miner is rewarded financially after triumphing mining of a block [11]. The third element is Node. Decentralization is one of the most significant topics in blockchain technology. The chain cannot be owned by a computer system or a company [12]. Rather, it is a disseminated ledger through which nodes are linked to the chain. Nodes (in any form of electronic gadget) manage replicas of the blockchain as well as maintains the functioning of the network. A major application of blockchain technology is to record financial information with a protected sharing. There have been efforts to enucleate as well as expand the usages of the blockchain technology beyond the field of finance and to other sectors namely, supply chain, automobile, healthcare, production, education, and banking [13]. It is widely classified that, “Blockchain 1.0” is typically linked with digital currency, for example, smart contracts, Bitcoin.“Blockchain 2.0” is connected with the help of automated digital finance processes, and also, the popular technology i.e. “Blockchain 3.0”. It is concentrated on considering the requirements of the digital community like smart homes, smart banking, smart cities, and Industry 4.0 [14]. The fourth industrial revolution is hugely powered by IoT and other associated technologies. Here is another concept of IIoT (Industrial Internet of Things) or Industrial IoT which has a more particular concentration while the fourth revolution of the industry is basically linked with an intelligent industry arrangement. The conversion from conventional industry to intelligent industry or IndustrialIoT is supported by the inter-associativity as well as the digitalization of our community and spreading of IoT gadgets [15].

The other sections of the chapter are organized as follows, Section 2 illustrated “Industry 4.0”, its evolution from Industry 1.0 to 4.0, “Industrial Internet of Things (IIoT)” and major differences between the fourth revolution of industry and IIoT. Then, in section 3, some major applications of blockchain with some primary issues are described in different sectors such as healthcare and agriculture along with some of the existing open challenges in adopting blockchain in the IIoT technology. Modern application sectors in Industrial IoT or Industry 4.0, where blockchain technology is being executed for finding plausible solutions, are described in section 4. Furthermore, sections 5 and 6 present some of the major challenges related to the implementation of IIoT, and solutions to deal with the implementation issues in detail. And finally, a conclusion is discussed in section 7.

2. BACKGROUND MATERIALS

This section discusses the Evolution of Industry 4.0, and the comparison between IIoT and IoT.

2.1. Industry 4.0

In every day of humans’ life, technology plays a significant role. It has made it plausible for data access to be much faster. The atmosphere of businesses is getting involved in this frequent evolution of technology. Gradually, the industrial field has been consolidating greater applications of automation and associativity. For 2020, “Industry 4.0” was proposed in the year 2011, at the Hanover event, Germany. In the revolution of industry, Industry 4.0 implies tobe the modern stage that concentrates mainly on inter-associativity, ML (machine learning), real-time data, and automation [16]. Also, it is sometimes implied as IIoT or smart production. Before going too much deeper, it is advantageous to first know how production has evolved since the 1800s. There are four different industrial revolutions as shown in (Fig. 1) that the world either has faced today [17].

Fig. (1))
Stages of revolution in industry.

2.1.1. The First Revolution of Industry

Between the late 1700s and the beginning of the 1800s, the first revolution of the industry occurred. During this time, production evolved from concentrating on manual exertion done by people and animals. It is supported by a more efficient type of exertion done by people through the usage of water as well as steam-powered engines and other kinds of machine weapons [18].

2.1.2. The Second Revolution of Industry

The world introduced a second revolution of the industry with the introduction of steel and the application of electricity in factories in the beginning stage of the 20th century. To raise the optimization and create factory machinery like mobile, electricity-enabled manufacturers were introduced in factories. And, during this stage, the concepts of mass manufacturing like the assembly line were proposed as a method to enhance productivity.

2.1.3. The Third Revolution of Industry

In the late 1950s, the third revolution of industry slowly started to rise as manufacturers and started unifying computer technology into their factories. During that period, they started experiencing a change that put less force on mechanical as well as analog technology, and more on digital technology including automation software.

2.1.4. The Fourth Revolution of Industry or Industry 4.0

From the last10 years, the fourth revolution of the industry has risen which is called “Industry 4.0”. It mainly focuses on digital technology from the current 10 years to a complete modern stage with the support of inter-associativity through the Internet of Things (IoT). A more interconnected, vast as well as holistic strategy is offered by Industry 4.0 to production [19]. It links physical with digital technology and permits for better optimization and access across sectors, products, and people. Also, business owners are empowered by Industry 4.0 to monitor and understand every facet of their implementation and permit them to leverage instant information to enhance manufacturing, boost operations and raise growth.

2.2. IIoT

IIoT implies the extension as well as usage of the IoT in industrial fields and applications. Including a large concentration on big data, machine learning, and M2M (machine-to-machine communication), IIoT provides enterprises and factories to have better optimization, capacity, and stability in their implementations. Also, it encircles industrial applications, including healthcare gadgets, software-defined manufacturing procedures, and robotics too [20]. Generally, IIoT is the usage of smart actuators as well as sensors to boost production and industrial procedures. Sometimes, it is also considered as the “Industry 4.0” or “Industrial Internet”. The main concept behind the IIoT is that smart machines are better than humans for evaluating data in real-time, and they are also preferred at interacting significant information that can be applied to make effective decisions faster and more precisely related to business [21].

2.2.1. IIoT versus IoT

Basically, both IIoT and IoT have many technologies that are known commonly, as M2M i.e. machine-to-machine communications, sensors and actuators, associativity, cloud platforms, and also, data analytics which can be applied for distinct objectives. The application of IoT is to link gadgets across many sectors namely, healthcare, enterprises, agriculture, automobile, customer and utilities, and also, including government and cities. Smart gadgets, fitness bands, and many other applications that normally don’t generate emergency circumstances if something goes amiss are included in IoT gadgets. While, on the other hand, the IIoT links gadgets as well as machines which further helps in production and utilities. System failures and downtime in deployments of the IIoT can produce high-risk cases or even life-threatening circumstances. The application of IIoT is more focused on enhancing capacity and increasing health or safety, in opposition to the user-concentrated nature of applications of IoT [22].

3. MAJOR APPLICATIONS

IIoT plays important role in today's world. The involvement of IoT with the industry makes it more popular as compared to other technologies. It is involved in different fields like the health industry, agriculture, and many more. The major applications of IIoT are explained as follows:

3.1. Healthcare Industry

In the healthcare industry, the sensitive information of patients can be shared in an efficient way by applying blockchain technology, which can effectively boost the distribution of medical services. For instance, by decreasing the efficiency for dissimilar patients and also, decreasing the number of errors that occur during patient care [23]. To facilitate the controlling as well as management of diseases, blockchain-powered IoT has been applied in the healthcare sector. Wearable gadgets, smart medicines, and enhanced quality control are included in some of the illustrations of blockchain-enabled IoT [24]. They also permit for tracing clinical medicines during their evaluation, by testing their impact as well as their negative effects.

Here are some main applications of blockchain technology in the industry of healthcare:

3.1.1. Patient Record Management

One of the major issues experienced in handling the record of the patient is that the patient leaves their medical associated information disseminated across several suppliers of healthcare. The composition of EHRs (Electronic Health Records) is not easy to handle and cannot scatter across several industrial setups. Moreover, the issues lie between several healthcare organizations as well as service suppliers who generate more obstacles for the victorious for exchanging of information [25]. This deficit of adjusted management of data as well as information sharing refers to that these medical records are not unified, but instead are segmented. Blockchain technology supports in giving a design for data exchanging along with the safety of this information. In this manner, medical care suppliers can collect details from the patients which may comprise their name, DOB (date of birth), prescription adhered, past processes performed, and other necessary details. By using cloud computing, this information is then recorded on the networks in the current databases of organizations. After that, from each source of information, a cryptographic hash of the information is computed and then, transferred along with the patient’s public ID which is to be recorded in the blockchain [26]. In this situation, smart contracts are a better option to handle the access of patient’s information. The medical care providers can query the blockchain and then, place this info through an API without exposing the identity of the patient.

3.1.2. Securing Patient Data

In the field of healthcare, the security of data is a primary concern. Keeping the prominent healthcare data of patients secure and protected is the most famous blockchain medical application at this moment. The criminal theft credit card-related information as well as banking data, and also, health and genomic testing records. The capability of blockchain is to keep an undisrupted, decentralized as well as clear log of all the data of the patients which makes this technology rife for safety applications [27]. Moreover, blockchain remains private while it is transparent, concealing the recognition of any character with complicated and safe codes that can secure the susceptibility of healthcare data. Because of the decentralization feature of blockchain, patients, doctors, and healthcare suppliers are permitted to exchange similar data quickly and securely.

3.1.3. Industrial Implementations

Following are some famous industrial implementations of blockchain in the medical care industry.

  1. MedRec: It is a famous instantiation of healthcare blockchain design for recording EHRs effectively as well as proficiently. The prototype of MedRec allows patients with one-stop-shop access to their healthcare history across several suppliers like smart contracts on an Ethereum blockchain aggregate data pointers into “patient-provider relationships”. Reviewing, sharing, and posting of new records, via an efficient and easy-to-use user interface, are facilitated by MedRec too [28]. The interface also includes a notification system to alert users or patients when new data has been uploaded on their behalf or shared with them. In this implementation of blockchain, the EHR also has an emerged blockchain PHR (i.e. Patient Health Record) ability that allows the user and the several service suppliers in creating longitudinal medical data consisting of whole life medical situations for each patient.
  2. Medicalchain: It is a blockchain for EHR (i.e. electronic health records) which allows several medical care providers namely, hospitals, surgeons, professionals, pharmacists as well as insurers to request permission to access and communicate with healthcare data. Each of the communications is safe, secure, auditable as well as transparent, and also it is stored as a transaction on Medicalchain’s disseminated ledger [29].

3.1.4. Challenges

Here are some of the complex issues that adopters in the healthcare industry could meet while applying blockchain into their systems.

  1. Healthcare is disseminated: Providers of healthcare services are not streamlined in reference to managing the data. Without such a system, it would be a complicated job to get them to apply blockchain technology. Moreover, if any of the participants in the system are resistive to these modifications, then the utility of the entire system goes down.
  2. Deficiency of approved reliability : Healthcare is such a delicate as well as a disseminated sector that can’t pass off modifications overnight. Thus, until the blockchain technology is triumphing implemented, it is tough to find if the acceptance of this technology will be feasible [30].
  3. Other Challenges: The implementation of blockchain technology needs the best architecture and inter-associativity, which startups cannot afford simply. Also, technical obstacles do not permit blockchain technology to apply to the healthcare sector completely. In some enterprises, the system of inter-linked gadgets is disrupted due to the scarcity of commands in the environment of blockchain.

3.2. Manufacturing Industry

The manufacturing sector is an essential participator in global GDP, which has always been persecuted with various challenges like inefficiency and data security. Though, similar to many industries, the manufacturing industry’s apparent health is not essentially indicative of forthcoming success. Blockchain has the ability to radically convert it by providing visibility across every asset of manufacturing [31]. Some use-cases of the blockchain in the industry of manufacturing are discussed below.

3.2.1. Enhanced Data Protection

Interacting information like contact and invoice details in any supply chain can be very breakneck using conventional procedures. This is where the blockchain aids to keep the details safe with the best cryptography methods [32]. Since, in blockchain, information blocks are nothing but orderly recorded replicas of documents that are connected to the preceding block, which eliminates the chance of data getting attacked.

3.2.2. Manufacturing Plants

For scheduled maintenance, the Internet of Things and Machine Learning technologies are already used by some manufacturing plants. The details associated with the lifecycle of machine parts can be collected and stored in a network of blockchain, and replacement parts can be ordered by smart machinery and paid on a scheduled basis. Moreover, producers can record their data in the blockchain network and apply it to link their ERP systems with their suppliers’ systems [33].

3.2.3. Developed Tracing as well as Traceability

An IoT-supported system can be implemented to trace the base of a manufacturing part. It improves transparency as well as traceability for everyone in the value chain. The data collected from IoT sensors can be recorded in the blockchain network, permitting for a shared view and also, assuring that there are no spaces in managing goods, as they move along with the supply chain [34]. Many processes included namely, documentations, getting certifications can also be enhanced significantly with the execution of the blockchain technology.

3.2.4. Credibility of Deliveries

Producers hugely depend on the quality of deliveries that they bought. Suppliers that are venerable, increasingly want their bands to be stored and popular. This requirement is confronted with the support of the blockchain where producers can get data related to their suppliers, so that, they confirm that they get what they claimed, and those deliveries are in the proper size, without any damage [35]. Also, the execution of smart contracts ensures the security of intellectual property as well as payments.

3.2.5. Efficient Inventory Management

The entries can be accessed by anyone in the network because blockchain technology allows the participants to link with every stakeholder in the supply chain, including suppliers, disseminators, or retailers. This implies, everyone holds a replica of the record, thereby avoiding confusion generated in the procedures of the supply chain. This aids to design and handle inventory superior.

3.2.6. Challenges

Blockchain has the ability to enhance various aspects of the manufacturing sector and make it more credible, cost-efficient, as well as fast. With a lot of advantages, several issues are also faced by the industries when it comes to leveraging blockchain technology into their manufacturing businesses. Business owners require to analyze the scalability of their businesses and to understand whether the acquisition of blockchain technology will be advantageous for them or not. Also, to get advantages from the blockchain in the manufacturing industry, it will need broad enhancement that will involve every participant of a chain. Each producer may be a segment of various blockchains, therefore, the setup required to handle all these chains is challenging. So, various executions of those blockchains have to be standardized as well as interoperable for a consolidated unification of these blockchains. Since various enterprises are generating possessory blockchains, interoperability would be a large issue to realize [36].

3.3. Agriculture Industry

Agriculture business relies on several exterior components (like temperature, climate, humidity, the perfection of reap, and many more), as well as complicated delivery chains that could be made circumstantially transparent by executing blockchain. This technology permits end-to-end transactions which occur transparently, without the requirement of an inter-medium such as a middleman or bank in the field of agriculture. By removing the requirement of a central organization, blockchain modifies the manner which granted faith– rather than banking organization. Faith is placed in cryptography as well as end-to-end infrastructure. It also helps to restore the faith between both the manufacturers and customers, which can decrease the costs of transactions in the agriculture market. A credible technique of tracking transactions between nameless participators is offered by the blockchain [37]. Blockchain supplies transparency among all involved communities, facilitates the set of credible information, and gives solutions to problems related to the quality of food and safety, which are hugely concerned by customers, organizations, and the government.

Some use-cases of blockchain technology in the agriculture sector are described below.

3.3.1. Production of Crop and Food

From farmer to producer and grocer, blockchain technology combined with the Internet of Things to reshape the food production market. Blockchain is ready to make farming a sustainable job by utilizing farming sources containing labor, water, as well as fertilizer, by implementing a simplified technique [38].

3.3.2. Preventing Weather Crisis

While producing distinct kinds of crops, farmers generally have to meet unpredictable weather circumstances. Thus, foreshowing as well as monitoring weather situations are necessary to crop production. Many of the crops produced in the United States (US) cannot endure due to intense spring rains. Also, lack of transparency in the recent food chain ecosystems can consequence in obscure and huge surge pricing. Customers do not have any idea when did the crops confront lousy weather circumstances and what led to the enhanced costs. Farmers, producers, and other stakeholders will be capable to get a straightforward understanding of the price distinctions in the market of food dissemination. From the blockchain ledger, authenticated organizations can track the weather situations, so that, farmers can quickly get the claims of crop insurance through smart contracts.

3.3.3. Food Supply Chain

Tracing a food supply chain is delicate to retracing the source from where the food has been produced. It is necessary to assure that the delivered food is safe to eat. But when it comes to the manner, the supply chain of food becomes an issue for the food originators as well as retailers to validate the origination of the products. With the integration of blockchain technology, it has become plausible to bring faith and transparency in the food delivery chain atmosphere, assuring food safety for each person.

3.3.4. Handling Agricultural Finance

Some of the innumerable issues occurring between formal financial meetings and smallholders are a shortage of transparency, credit histories, and problems in contract enforcement. The incapability to get financial services can have an unfavorable influence on the performance of agricultural value chains due to which manufacturers cannot increase their yields and purchasers meet problems [39]. Blockchain generates fairness in the procedure of agricultural finance via transparency as well as shared control accessibility.

3.3.5. Challenges

The information entering into the chain of blocks is assumed to be transparent as well as public, and it could be an issue to persuade and embolden small-scale consumers to accept the concept of blockchain technology. Most of the existing solutions are based on this gathered data through IoT sensors. Hence, additionally, paying for the subscription of the blockchain, the included organizations have to compensate for the implementation of the Internet of Things. Secondly, collecting the info uploaded to a blockchain platform can be very expensive, which will be an obstacle to the acceptance of blockchain in the agriculture industry. Thirdly, a blockchain does not straightly seamlessly combine with current legacy systems and constructing a structure to apply the blockchain technology, which is often time-consuming. Thus, the complications present in the blockchain will be a preservative to the illustration of solutions based on this technology in the agricultural field.

4. OPEN ISSUES AND MODERN APPLICATION SECTORS

In this part of the chapter, some of the recent major issues in the acquisition of blockchain in the Industrial Internet of Things technology get discussed along with modern application sectors in Industrial IoT or Industry 4.0 where blockchain technology is being executed for finding plausible solutions.

4.1. Open Challenges related to Adoption of Blockchain Technology in IIoT

The Open Challenges related to Adoption of Blockchain Technology in IIoT are explained as follows:

4.1.1. Energy and Cost Efficiency

As the size of blockchain gets increases, more powerful miners have to be appointed for implementing the consensus mechanism such as Proof-of-Work. Rather than recording the complete data of blockchain, some energy-adept protocols of consensus can record only the most current data of transactions on the blockchain. However, still, energy and resource-constrained industrial IoT gadgets are overpowered by large amounts of commercial information produced [40]. Hence, the structure of more adept consensus algorithms is still a major issue.

4.1.2. Regulations

A central organization, commercial standard as well as government regulations must be enforced since the blockchain technology implements in a decentralized manner without depending on any kind of the third party.

4.1.3. Resource Constraints

Existing blockchain workings needed constant network connectivity, and the gadgets of industrial IoT did not always ensure long-lasting network connections. Hence, the execution of blockchain is an issue in this situation. Moreover, high network overhead is incurred by the blockchain which is again a problem for unification into industrial IoT [41]. Due to the resource extents of IoT gadgets as well as networks, the degree of decentralization plausible in existing executions of blockchain technology combined with the IoT networks [42].

4.1.4. Security and Confidentiality

Already, IoT networks are prone to several protection perpetrations namely, eavesdropping perpetrations and replay perpetrations. Along with these threats, blockchains bring several safety risks. Some of the familiar safety threats in the technology are message hijack, confidentiality leakage, smart contract program risks, etc.

4.1.5. Scalability

In the blockchain, the problem of scalability is a primary disincentive in broad compliance. Each completely taking part as a node that is needed to hold a replica of blockchain in existing executions of blockchain technology. In opposition to conventional databases, this moves to large storage overhead [43]. Particularly, in the case of industrial IoT, the number of sensor nodes as well as the amount of information produced get increasing, therefore the issue becomes aggravated.

4.2. Modern Application Fields in IIoT where blockchain is being implemented

In IIoT, there are various application fields where blockchain is being implemented as explained below.

4.2.1. Taxi Industry

Existing services of ride-sharing like Uber, Ola operate with a concentrated structure, implementing algorithms from their dispatch centers to monitor their fleet of cabs. The blockchain technology could supply a decentralized as well as disseminated technique to the current model with cab drivers and riders for producing a more consumer-driven customer location. To work on a huger scale, through faith and better coverage, drivers can also cooperate. They can also form a repeating list of clients whom they can depend upon for daily income, and the consumers will have a river that they can bank upon.

4.2.2. Music Industry

The integrity of a copyrighted product is assured by a smart contract. These smart contracts describe and automate the communications as well as connections between the stakeholder of a product. Also, it helps in assuring that the product, a consumer is purchasing, is authorized, and not just an exquisite replica. In the sector of music, music rights management is an efficient use case of smart contracts [44]. Because of the Internet improvement as well as numerous online music streaming features in the past 10-15 years, the music industry has been experiencing a primary modification. The procedure of royalty assessment in the music industry has always been a complex job. However, in the development of Internet services, this method has become even more complicated [45]. Blockchain can assist to make the royalty payment procedure secure as well as transparent by handling a precise and extensive decentralized database for recording complete data (in a disseminated ledger manner) of music rights ownership.

4.2.3. Education Industry

The potency of distance learning based on the web helps in the requirement for a self-reliant as well as the transparent manner of checking up educational data and transcripts of students. For educational documents, a platform based on blockchain technology could work as a scrivener and can produce a form for academic organizations and proprietors to get safe transcripts as well as all the recorded data. The circumstantially functional characteristic of employment indicates that more specializations are needed as career paths digress across institutions and disciplines [46]. Now, in this scenario, blockchain can become the base of academic proof, providing proprietors information required to find whether the CV of an applicant is precise or not.

4.2.4. Drone Industry

The drone industry is the modern industry where blockchain technology is being attempted as well as tested. Drones (also known as UAVs i.e. Unmanned Aerial Vehicles) are being appointed on a huge scale for civilian motives, digressing from their conventional usage in the military sector. Safety of UAV network is a significant field, with a lot of research works in this sector. In a study [47], a combination of blockchain technology with UAV networks is proposed, where each unmanned aerial vehicle in the network will operate as a node of the blockchain. This proposal is considered to secure the network from intrusion

perpetrations where the perpetrators attempt to intercept interactions as well as the control commands to take control of each and every node in the network [48].

5. CHALLENGES IMPLEMENTATION

The physical world is converted into computerized. An eruption of smart gadgets and modern technologies has enabled humans to be in frequent interaction with one another anytime. Huge volumes of information are simply handled as well as evaluated with data metric technology. Out of modern as well as trending technologies, one such technology is the Industrial of Things (IIoT) which is also called Industry 4.0 or Industrial Internet. The technology can introduce amazing gadgets, enhanced analytics, and the people embraced at work. On concatenating M2M interaction with big data analytics in the industry, Industrial IoT can run unexcelled stages of optimization, producibility, and outcomes. Almost all the companies are refracted towards the Internet of Things enhancement organizations to achieve the technology unified into their works. With the advancement of the IIoT, it has become demonstrable in handling real-time dynamics of data analytics in the commercial field. However, there are some challenges that are holding the implementation of the industrial internet of things. The major challenges recently confronted by industries are illustrated in (Fig. 2).

Fig. (2))
Challenges related to IIoT implementation.

5.1. Data Storage

Data storage is one of the primary challenges for businesses. In today’s world, all presumed activities largely depend on the saved data from the past. No organization lives with an ancient conventional procedure to unravel the data which typically would be evaluated high-frequency info, and then throw it away [49]. IIoT helps to gather a large amount of data that have sensitive pertinency to forthcoming assets of the enterprise outside of the OT network. Thus, it becomes a requirement for any business to prearrange for safe storage of info before going through the complete industrial internet of things in the long run [50].

5.2. Analytics Challenge

In this challenge, if the internet of things solution is executed in the business, its original ROI value is determined through actionable insights implied from the gathered IoT content. This could only be plausible with the support of a high-performance analytics platform that can manage the huge amount of info included to the solution. It is necessary for the data analytics team to include processing, cleaning, as well as representation of data while executing the structure of IoT. This ascertains leaving sufficient space for extendibility component to concatenate real-time or predictive analytics simply to a solution of IoT [51].

5.3. Connectivity Outage Challenge

There is a stable requirement for undisturbed connectivity if an organization is thinking to go industrial IoT. Even while using the Internet connection, its presence of 100% is approximately impracticable. At one point in time, the connectivity is lost either for maintenance purposes or for some other purpose [52]. Hence, if an organization is thinking to bring IIoT technology into their systems, then the crucial requirement is to be available with an uninterrupted connection.

5.4. Delivering Value to the Customer

The design to execute solutions of industrial IoT can hugely influence capacity, consumer gratification, as well as productivity in the long run. Having industrial IoT is a huge deal and the complete process requires deep understanding. Businesses generally design for modern technology to bridge the space of understanding the consumers’ problem statement. Thus, it becomes highly necessary for consultants of the internet of things to find out the major performance pointers to measure and enhance through an IoT solution [53].

5.5. Security

In history, there had been several cases of cyber-perpetrations recorded. Thus, it is so much necessary to protect sensitive information from cyber crimes than handling general networks of IT. And, hence, this had been the hugest challenge of the industrial internet of things for the implementation as well as technology, since a usual risk can destroy the business [54]. In case, if an enterprise prearranges to reduce such problems with industrial IoT [55], this would mean proposing new protection tools to the network which implies progressed cost as well as heavy maintenance. Hence, many enterprises are generally avoiding the concept of the industrial internet of things until they are prepared with a solid security or protection arrangement in place [56].

6. SOLUTIONS TO DEAL WITH INDUSTRIAL IOT IMPLEMENTATION CHALLENGES

To deal with the challenges of industrial internet of things implementation, there are various solutions, as presented in (Fig. 3), that businesses, organizations, or companies can apply in their systems.

Fig. (3))
Solutions to handle IIoT implementation challenges.

6.1. Clustering of IoT Gateway

Internet of Things gateway grouping assures the unification of IT systems, namely ERP systems and CRM applications with OT systems like SCADA and MES systems. Also, it assures the consistency of cloud interaction and recording odd info, which resolves the issue of OT as well as IT convergence [57].

6.2. TTM, TPM and TNM

To reduce security problems, commercial units can bring into operation trusted terminal module (i.e. TTM), trusted perception module (i.e. TPM), and trusted network module (i.e. TNM). Additionally, there are numerous data concentrated protection solutions that assure the security of data encryption while in transit state or in a rest state, which consists of Safe Web Gateway, Web Application Firewall, and also, Application Transmission Controller.

6.3. IIoT Gateway

Industrial internet of things gateway can support the current gadget architecture to safely link to any commercial architecture. For example, a gateway of IoT can link the disseminated control systems straightly with the cloud platform using industrial protocols namely, OPC, wireless technology, MODBUS, ISA100, and PROFIBUS for edge to gateway connection. This works as a solution for the issue of device-to-device interaction [58].

6.4. Edge Computing

Edge computing technology permits only reasonable data to transmit further for analytics rather than transferring a group of data on the cloud platform. In edge computing technology, a particular number of gateways having distinct operations are linked with each other to generate a bunch of gateways and this bunch leads to disseminated edge computing [59]. In this, disseminated edge nodes permit the processing of data near the edge as well as near the source before transferring it to the cloud, which decreases delay as an output [60]. After that, all the filtered info can be straightly transmitted to the fog node and conjugation of fog nodes permit disseminated fog computing. This will assist in speedy data sharing, real-time data evaluation, and allowing faster defect response time.

7. CONCLUSION

As a result of the forthcoming fourth revolution of industry, also called Industry 4.0, modern deleterious technologies are being presumed to be consolidated in the atmosphere of businesses. One of these solutions is “Blockchain technology”, which targets to unify heterogeneous systems and handle industrial transactions. While blockchain technology plays an important character, it is delicate for the holistic enhancement of Industrial IoT or Industry 4.0. IIoT endeavors for huger amounts of automation in modern society. According to experts, IIoT or Industry 4.0 technology will definitely develop production stages even further with time. It is considered that industrial IoT will ultimately become the driving force of innovations behind several kinds of revolution. In this chapter, we have discussed the applications of blockchain in IIoT in detail along with the detailed presentation of Industry 4.0 and IIoT. Furthermore, challenges associated with the implementations of IIoT and solutions to deal with those challenges are also illustrated.

Enhancement of Iot Security Solutions Using Blockchain Technology



K. Sheela1, *, C. Priya2
1 Department of Computer Science, Vels Institute of Science, Technology and Advanced Studies (VISTAS), Chennai, India
2 Department of Information Technology, Vels Institute of Science, Technology and Advanced Studies (VISTAS), Chennai, India

Abstract

A blockchain is a growing list of records, called blocks, which are linked together using cryptography. Each block contains a cryptographic hash of the previous block, a timestamp, and transaction data. It is a decentralized, distributed and public digital ledger that is used to record transactions across many computers called nodes. Blockchain, in collaboration with the technologies like Artificial Intelligence, the Internet of Things, Big data, etc., provides more effective outcomes by minimizing manpower with an increase in its feasibility. IoT is all about expanding the power of the internet beyond computers and smartphones to a whole range of other things, processes, and environments. Our day-to-day life can be made interesting with the applications of IoT-enabled devices in the home, agriculture, logistics, industries, healthcare sectors, and so on. They can be combined with the disciplines like data collection, management of huge data, storage of data, data analytics, etc. These IoT devices are embedded with sensors that are vulnerable to hackers and malware. At present, we cannot give complete assurance of the security of the access and data associated with the devices as it is possible for an intruder to misuse or mishandle the scheduled process. But, in association with blockchain technology, we can acquire solutions for problems related to security.

Keywords: Application, Authenticity, Blockchain, Banking sector, Consensus, Corda, Crypto-currency, Cryptography, Distributed, Ether, Ethereum, E-governance, Hashing, Healthcare, IoT, Platforms, Proof of work and stack, Security, Smart contract, Smart city.

* Corresponding author K. Sheela: Department of Computer Science, Vels Institute of Science, Technology and Advanced Studies (VISTAS), Chennai, India; E-mail: ksheela.research@gmail.com

1. INTRODUCTION

IoT devices have become a regular component in our day-to-day life. We can see that the devices which are connected to the internet are gradually increasing by

paving the way for a digital environment. The traditional database provides a centralized architecture, whereas blockchain provides a distributed peer-to-peer network. In simple terms, blockchain is a collection of data stored in a database that maintains the continuously growing records in a distributed, peer-to-peer network node [1]. This makes the Blockchain and IoT to get integrated to provide even better services. Generally, IoT devices are liable to many kinds of security attacks [2]. The following are some of the attacks which occur to the devices connected to the internet.

Brute Force Attack: It is the process of trying all the possibilities of the password given by the authorized user.

The Man in the Middle: In this kind of cyber-attack, an intruder involves himself in the communication and gain access to the messages transferred. It is also possible for him to alter the messages which lead to miscommunication.

Denial of Service: This attack is used to slow down the process which also affects the reputation of an organization. Distributed Denial of Service (DDoS) may also occur to engage the resources in unnecessary services and makes the network resources unavailable for the appropriate activities. One of the methods to overcome this is proof of Activity which is the combination of PoS and PoW [3].

Eavesdropping: It is also known as sniffing or snooping attacks. It is the process of stealing the information while it gets transmitted over the network by devices like a smartphone, computers, or other connected devices.

Sybil Attack: It is the process of creating large numbers of pseudonymous identities to hide real identities and slow down the process. It entirely damages the operation of the network.

These attacks can be overridden by collaborating blockchain technology with the Internet of Things having the features like decentralized framework, immutability, reliability, autonomous behavior, and so on [4]. Every transaction in the blockchain is cryptographically sealed using public/private keys. Every transaction must be defined and authenticated. With these transactions, blocks must be created, validated, and linked (chained) [5] to enhance the security level of the transactions.

Blockchain technology, in collaboration with technologies like Artificial Intelligence, Internet of Things [IOT], Bigdata, etc. provides more effective end results by minimizing the manpower with an increase in its feasibility. Certainly, Blockchain entitles IoT devices by providing security and authentication with transparency in IoT ecosystems. We can easily predict that IoT will become a part of our day-to-day life in our near future with its huge development and collaboration with effective technologies like blockchain technology. Fraudulent activities may also occur in the localization of IoT devices where the location of the connected device might be tampered with to provide erroneous data. It can be overridden by this blockchain technology [2]. It is well-known specifically for its unique features like decentralized and distributed nature, hashing and cryptographic techniques, and smart contracts maintained between them. Let us have a deep look into this collaboration in this chapter.

2. PLATFORMS TO IMPLEMENT BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY AND ITS USES

By considering the nature of the Blockchain, it has been classified into three types, namely private, public, and consortium. A private blockchain is a restricted one. The user, who can access the data, will be assigned by a network administrator and others will be restricted from accessing it. There is no restriction of access in the public blockchain. The consortium is a semi-restricted blockchain where access permission will be given with certain restrictions to work with the blockchain platform [6]. A platform is an environment in which a piece of software can be executed. Many platforms have been designed to support and develop blockchain technology. These platforms are classified into two categories, namely permissioned and permission-less: The following are some of the most effectively used platforms for this technology:

Ethereum– It is a permission-less, decentralized application (DApp). It uses Ether as its cryptocurrency (ETH).

Hyperledger fabric – It is a permissioned blockchain where only known identities could be able to participate.

Ripple – It is most essentially designed for transactions on payment.

Quorum – It is an enterprise-focused version of ethereum.

Corda – Initially, it was designed for the financial sector. But nowadays, it has also been implemented in other sectors too.

EOS – It eliminates fees for all of its users.

Open chain – It was highly designed for managing digital assets.

Stellar – It deals with all the transactions and exchanges between cryptocurrencies and fiat-based currencies. To achieve the desired consensus, it uses Stellar Consensus Protocol (SCP).

There are many other types of platforms available to implement blockchain technology. The above mentioned are the platforms that are highly popular and user-friendly platforms of this technology. Table (1) provides a clear comparison between the top 5 blockchain platforms in the most efficient forums [7].
